# Route LI producer loop (2026-10-13)

- **ID:** li-producer-loop-1013
- **Stage:** 1-in-progress
- **Type:** CONJECTURE LOOP
- **Statement:** produce an unconditional subexponential lower bound for the actual Li clock, or create the missing mechanism.

## Core in corpus notation (read from Lean, not from old notes)

- `Φ(x) = ξ(½+x)/ξ(½) = Σ c_j x^{2j}`, `c_0 = 1`, `c_j > 0` (`LiClockThetaReciprocalSource`).
- Borel kernel `B(t) = Σ c_j t^{2j}/(2j)!`, `K = B'` (odd entire, positive Taylor coefficients).
- Clock primitive `P(t) = Σ_q (1 − cos(t w_q))`, `w_q = 1/(−i(ρ_q − ½))` (`LiEvenCrossActualClockPrimitive`).
- Exact Volterra law `P + K⋆P = (t/2)K` (factor-one form in `LiEvenCrossActualClockPrimitiveVolterra`).
- Laplace form: `P̂(p) = −½ (d/dp) log Φ(1/p)`.
- RH ⟺ `P ≥ 0` on `[0,∞)` ⟺ `P ≥ −Ce^{εt}` ∀ε (`growthRate_le_iff_liEvenCrossActualClock_lower`).
- First-crossing barrier: `LiEvenCrossActualClockHistoryBarrier` reduces positivity of `D = ∫P/s` to the
  outside-correlation budget `Cout(t) ≤ tK(t) + (K(t)/2)∫_0^{e(t)} D` at hypothetical first zeros after 20.

## Representation adopted in this loop: infinite divisibility

`L[δ₀ + K](p) = Φ(1/p)`, so with `M := δ₀ + K(t)dt`:

**RH ⟺ M is infinitely divisible on [0,∞) (Lévy density `2P(t)/t ≥ 0`) ⟺ `p ↦ log Φ(1/p)` is completely monotone.**

`Φ(x) = E cosh(xU)`, where `U` is distributed according to the normalized Riemann kernel. So `M` is the scale mixture
`E_U[M_{|U|}]` of the cosh-laws `M_u` (`L[M_u](p) = cosh(u/p)`), and each `M_u` is ID. RH is the statement
that this particular scale mixture of ID laws remains ID.

Arithmetic split, with `s = ½ + 1/p`: the archimedean factor `Γ(s/2)` supplies the decreasing (CM-direction) part.
Every Euler factor contributes `q^{−k/2} e^{−k log q/p}/k`, which increases in `p`, so it is anti-CM.
Its inverse Laplace transform is the signed Bessel term `−√(c/t)J₁(2√(ct))`. The balance is wall 1 in Bessel form.

The decisive question is a spectral argument for the degenerate operator `E² + 4μ₁μ₂∂₁ − (μ₁²+4μ₂²)` with drift.

## Session synthesis (2026-10-14), the laws found in cycles 16–62

1. **Hardy projection (MUST-1398, [L]).** Cauchy center-averaging projects on-line clock atoms onto their decaying half. Off-line atoms keep both exponentials, which gives depth-free detection.
2. **Euler shadow (MUST-1404, [L]).** The projected clock `F` is Euler data: `L[F](p) = Re ξ'/ξ(3/2+1/p)/p²`. RH ⟺ `A ≡ F`.
3. **One-sided Laplace law (MUST-1405, [L]).** Each off-line reflection pair contributes exactly `Re(−2/D) < 0`. RH ⟺ `L[A](p) ≥ L[F](p)` at a single `p ≥ 1`. Slope `A'(0⁺)` vs `Re ξ'/ξ(3/2)` is the `p → ∞` form ([L], row 21b).
4. **Exactness principle (row 33).** The Weil margin is `≤ exp(−πe^{2L})` and the clock deficit is `≈ 4/γ²` (depth-free), so arbitrarily small. Producers must be exact identities or Gram realizations, never estimates.
5. **Poisson-exactness law (row 40).** Every Dirichlet polynomial (finite prime data) has zero center-averaged defect. RH is the commutation of `X → ∞` with line means.
6. **Clock prime-blindness law (row 35).** A Li-type test is prime-blind iff its Mellin symbol decays integrably and is analytic right of its singularity. All content then sits in the central residue (theta cumulants).
7. **ρ-shift law (row 54).** For every Eisenstein family of rank ≥ 1, ζ-zeros sit at a fixed nonzero rational distance from the unitary axis. Only GL(1) has zero shift (Connes's spectral realization).
8. **Interlacing law for homotopies (row 57).** A convex homotopy from any crystalline real-rooted reference to Ξ needs `\|N − N_ref\| ≤ 1`, which fails because `S(T)` is unbounded.
9. **Terminal law (row 30, corpus).** Ξ is terminal for every reality-creating flow (heat, differentiation, `cos(hD)`).
10. **Hypertranscendence kill (row 53).** No finite-order positive recurrence or ODE producer exists for Li/ξ data.
11. **Unit-circle dictionary (row 48).** Zeros of Φ in its strip are critical points of `x^{3/2}ψ'(x)` with modular inversion. The natural boundary breaks full-strip criteria.
12. **Two-term Pólya PDE (row 49).** `−z²W = [E² + 4μ₁μ₂∂₁ − (μ₁²+4μ₂²)]W`, with a degenerate principal symbol. This is the open spectral problem behind any Pólya-type extension.
13. **Local-vs-global law (rows 36, 41, 42, 55).** Hecke, Petersson, arithmetic Hodge index and amplification over arithmetic bases all give local (Ramanujan/central) positivity. Global zeros need cohomology of the base `Spec ℤ` (the F₁ object).

**Row 49 continued (e): large-`b` saddle analysis supports the conjecture for every ratio `κ = a/b`.** With `z = bζ` and `U = κ cosh t + cosh 2t`, the saddles solve `sinh t(κ + 4cosh t) = iζ`. The integrand decays at both ends only for `\|Im t\| ≤ π/4` (`Re cosh 2t = cosh 2x cos 2y`), so only saddles in that strip are relevant. For `ζ < g(π/4) = κ/√2 + 2` the single relevant saddle is imaginary (`t = iy₁`, `g(y) = sin y(κ+4cos y)` increasing on `(0, π/4)`). It contributes a real positive term, so there are no zeros in that range. Beyond, saddles come in mirror pairs `t, −t̄` (since `U'(−t̄) = −conj U'(t)`), giving conjugate contributions and a real oscillation, hence real zeros. The coalescence region is Airy-type, again with real zeros. The cycle-48 threshold `κ = 4` (imaginary critical points of `V`) does not appear, because those critical points lie outside the decay strip. **Prediction: two-term Pólya holds for all `a, b > 0`, and the (T) hypothesis is not sharp.** A proof needs a uniform version of this saddle picture down to bounded `b` (computation-free route not found). **Row 49 continued (f): shift-flow and Fourier projection.**
- **Shift flow (exact):** `∂_a W_{a,b} = −cos(D)W_{a,b}`, with `cos(D)W(z) = (W(z+i)+W(z−i))/2`. Hence `W_{a,b} = e^{−a cos D}W_{0,b}`, where `W_{0,b}(z) = K_{iz/2}(b)` has real zeros (Pólya).
- **Zero motion:** a simple real zero moves by `dz_j/da = −Re W(z_j+i)/W'(z_j)`, which is real. So reality can fail only at a collision of real zeros. Unlike the heat flow (Calogero repulsion), the velocity is nonlocal (values at `z_j ± i`), so no repulsion mechanism is visible.
- **Polynomial warning:** `(1 − ε cos D)(z² − c²)` has imaginary zeros once `ε(1+c²) > c²`. So `e^{−a cos D}` is not a general LP-preserver; any proof must use that the Bessel zeros stay far from the origin.
- **Fourier projection:** `W(z) = (1/2π)∫_ℝ Ĝ(z−2w, w)dw` with `Ĝ = 4K_{iz}(a)K_{iw}(b)`, which is nonvanishing off the real axis in each variable (product of Pólya functions). But projecting a two-variable "stable" function along a real line is a convolution, and convolutions of LP functions are not LP. This is circular back to the product-kernel problem.
- Status: OPEN. The decisive missing piece is a collision-free mechanism for the nonlocal shift flow restricted to Bessel inputs.

**Row 70 continued (C70b): factorized proof strategy for "1/ξ(½+u) is positive-definite".**
- **Factorization:** `ξ(½)/ξ(½+u) = Π_on(1+u²/γ²)^{−1} · Π_off F_q(u)`, with `F_q = \|w\|⁴/\|w²−u²\|²` for `w = δ+iγ`. So `f = f_on ⋆ f_off`.
- **On-line part:** `f_on` is the PF∞ density of `Σ_on L_γ/γ`. It is log-concave with `\|(log f_on)'\| ≤ γ_min^on`. If the lowest zero is on-line, then `f_on(t−s)/f_on(t) ∈ [e^{−γ₁\|s\|}, e^{γ₁\|s\|}]`.
- **Off-line factor (exact):** `FT[F_q](t) = (π\|w\|²/(2γδ))e^{−γ\|t\|}(γ sin(δ\|t\|) + δ cos(δ\|t\|))`. It is positive on `\|t\| < (π − arctan(δ/γ))/δ ≥ 2π − o(1)` and changes sign only beyond.
- **Sufficient condition:** `f(t) ≥ f_on(t)[∫e^{−γ₁\|s\|}df_off⁺ − ∫e^{γ₁\|s\|}df_off⁻]`. The negative tails carry weight `≈ e^{−(γ_q−γ₁)π/δ_q}`. With the band (`γ_q ≥ 14.68`, gap `≥ 0.55` above `γ₁ ≈ 14.13`, `δ < ½`) this is `≲ 0.03` per factor for the lowest hypothetical off-line zeros, versus a positive weighted mass `(γ/2)(2γ+γ₁)/(γ+γ₁)²` per factor (`→ 1` as `γ → ∞`, `≈ 0.375` near `γ₁`).
- **Status:** plausible but quantitative. It needs a bound on the number of hypothetical off-line zeros at low height (explicit `N(T)`) and on `γ₁`'s isolation, i.e. explicit zero counting near height 15 (computation; rule 3).
- **Conjecture C70 (restated):** `ξ(½)/ξ(½+u)` is a characteristic function on ℝ unconditionally. Under RH it is the characteristic function of `Σ_γ L_γ/γ`.

## Session synthesis II (2026-10-14, rows 74–93)

New Lean: MUST-1410, `xiOfflineInverseSquareMass_bounds_laplaceGap` (depth-free off-line counter, two-sided).

Laws:
1. **Light-cone separability** (74). Parameter self-adjointness with a scalar potential exists only for single-cosh kernels; multi-saddle kernels give shift couplings.
2. **Archimedean cone** (75). Function-field Weil functionals are atomic on log prime powers; ζ's archimedean and pole parts are continuous (`q ≥ 2` floor).
3. **Refined door-2** (77, 82). Depth-free global counters exist (MUST-1410); the obstruction is evaluation, not definability.
4. **Robust Lee–Yang class `Λ = −∞`** (79). It is product-closed via Newman coupling and a diagonal limit. **Fold law** (heuristic): fold ⇒ real zeros, crossover ⇒ complex zeros; it predicts `Λ(e^{−a cosh t}) = −a/2`.
5. **Landau size duality** (80). Positivity of `G/ζ` needs `G` at least ζ-sized on primes; known nonvanishing needs `G` to be `o(1)` there.
6. **Displacement dictionary** (84). Non-temperedness displaces zeros by inheritance; ζ's zeros are emergent.
7. **Pressure wall** (86) and **capacity wall** (88). Dynamics and elementary auxiliary integers reach only the absolute-convergence / sub-PNT regime.
8. **Concavity + exact quadratic identities = NB** (87).
9. **LI four-view dictionary** (89). Volterra lane, compound-Poisson criterion, C70 and Riemann-spin Lee–Yang are one object; C70 is strictly weaker than the budget.
10. **Counting–Weil split** (91). Weil negativity can occur only on tests blind to the height-counting measure, i.e. (N1) witnesses.
11. **Depth-exponent law** (92) and **near-line law** (93). Euler-evaluable ⟹ exponent 2. Exponents 0 and 1 need contour data on `Re s = ½ ± ε`, `ε → 0`.

Conclusion for route LI: every LI statement is a receiver whose evaluation factors through near-line contour data. A producer must be an exact exponent-2 identity, i.e. a Gram/Hodge realization of the Weil form. Route LI therefore terminates at wall 5 (the HF route's open core, with (N1) as its sharpest finite form).

In Montgomery's language this is the form factor range `α ∈ [1, √2]` (Goldston–Montgomery equivalence with the prime variance in short intervals). A zero-blind producer would need its weighted primes to ANTI-correlate at those scales enough to pull the variance below budget², i.e. a weighted-prime measure with sub-Poissonian pair correlation in intervals of length `x^{0.293}`.
